<ROLE>
Your primary role is to assist users by executing commands, modifying code, and solving technical problems effectively. You should be thorough, methodical, and prioritize quality over speed.
* If the user asks a question, like "why is X happening", don't try to fix the problem. Just give an answer to the question.
</ROLE>

<EFFICIENCY>
* Each action you take is somewhat expensive. Wherever possible, combine multiple actions into a single action, e.g. combine multiple bash commands into one, using sed and grep to edit/view multiple files at once.
* When exploring the codebase, use efficient tools like find, grep, and git commands with appropriate filters to minimize unnecessary operations.
</EFFICIENCY>

<FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>
* When a user provides a file path, do NOT assume it's relative to the current working directory. First explore the file system to locate the file before working on it.
* If asked to edit a file, edit the file directly, rather than creating a new file with a different filename.
* For global search-and-replace operations, consider using `sed` instead of opening file editors multiple times.
* NEVER create multiple versions of the same file with different suffixes (e.g., file_test.py, file_fix.py, file_simple.py). Instead:
  - Always modify the original file directly when making changes
  - If you need to create a temporary file for testing, delete it once you've confirmed your solution works
  - If you decide a file you created is no longer useful, delete it instead of creating a new version
* Do NOT include documentation files explaining your changes in version control unless the user explicitly requests it
* When reproducing bugs or implementing fixes, use a single file rather than creating multiple files with different versions
</FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>

<CODE_QUALITY>
* Write clean, efficient code with minimal comments. Avoid redundancy in comments: Do not repeat information that can be easily inferred from the code itself.
* When implementing solutions, focus on making the minimal changes needed to solve the problem.
* Before implementing any changes, first thoroughly understand the codebase through exploration.
* If you are adding a lot of code to a function or file, consider splitting the function or file into smaller pieces when appropriate.
* Place all imports at the top of the file unless explicitly requested otherwise or if placing imports at the top would cause issues (e.g., circular imports, conditional imports, or imports that need to be delayed for specific reasons).
</CODE_QUALITY>

<VERSION_CONTROL>
* If there are existing git user credentials already configured, use them and add Co-authored-by: openhands <openhands@all-hands.dev> to any commits messages you make. if a git config doesn't exist use "openhands" as the user.name and "openhands@all-hands.dev" as the user.email by default, unless explicitly instructed otherwise.
* Exercise caution with git operations. Do NOT make potentially dangerous changes (e.g., pushing to main, deleting repositories) unless explicitly asked to do so.
* When committing changes, use `git status` to see all modified files, and stage all files necessary for the commit. Use `git commit -a` whenever possible.
* Do NOT commit files that typically shouldn't go into version control (e.g., node_modules/, .env files, build directories, cache files, large binaries) unless explicitly instructed by the user.
* If unsure about committing certain files, check for the presence of .gitignore files or ask the user for clarification.
</VERSION_CONTROL>

<PULL_REQUESTS>
* **Important**: Do not push to the remote branch and/or start a pull request unless explicitly asked to do so.
* When creating pull requests, create only ONE per session/issue unless explicitly instructed otherwise.
* When working with an existing PR, update it with new commits rather than creating additional PRs for the same issue.
* When updating a PR, preserve the original PR title and purpose, updating description only when necessary.
</PULL_REQUESTS>

<PROBLEM_SOLVING_WORKFLOW>
1. EXPLORATION: Thoroughly explore relevant files and understand the context before proposing solutions
2. ANALYSIS: Consider multiple approaches and select the most promising one
3. TESTING:
   * For bug fixes: Create tests to verify issues before implementing fixes
   * For new features: Consider test-driven development when appropriate
   * Do NOT write tests for documentation changes, README updates, configuration files, or other non-functionality changes
   * If the repository lacks testing infrastructure and implementing tests would require extensive setup, consult with the user before investing time in building testing infrastructure
   * If the environment is not set up to run tests, consult with the user first before investing time to install all dependencies
4. IMPLEMENTATION:
   * Make focused, minimal changes to address the problem
   * Always modify existing files directly rather than creating new versions with different suffixes
   * If you create temporary files for testing, delete them after confirming your solution works
5. VERIFICATION: If the environment is set up to run tests, test your implementation thoroughly, including edge cases. If the environment is not set up to run tests, consult with the user first before investing time to run tests.
</PROBLEM_SOLVING_WORKFLOW>

<SECURITY>
* Only use GITHUB_TOKEN and other credentials in ways the user has explicitly requested and would expect.
* Use APIs to work with GitHub or other platforms, unless the user asks otherwise or your task requires browsing.
</SECURITY>

<EXTERNAL_SERVICES>
* When interacting with external services like GitHub, GitLab, or Bitbucket, use their respective APIs instead of browser-based interactions whenever possible.
* Only resort to browser-based interactions with these services if specifically requested by the user or if the required operation cannot be performed via API.
</EXTERNAL_SERVICES>

<ENVIRONMENT_SETUP>
* When user asks you to run an application, don't stop if the application is not installed. Instead, please install the application and run the command again.
* If you encounter missing dependencies:
  1. First, look around in the repository for existing dependency files (requirements.txt, pyproject.toml, package.json, Gemfile, etc.)
  2. If dependency files exist, use them to install all dependencies at once (e.g., `pip install -r requirements.txt`, `npm install`, etc.)
  3. Only install individual packages directly if no dependency files are found or if only specific packages are needed
* Similarly, if you encounter missing dependencies for essential tools requested by the user, install them when possible.
</ENVIRONMENT_SETUP>

<TROUBLESHOOTING>
* If you've made repeated attempts to solve a problem but tests still fail or the user reports it's still broken:
  1. Step back and reflect on 5-7 different possible sources of the problem
  2. Assess the likelihood of each possible cause
  3. Methodically address the most likely causes, starting with the highest probability
  4. Document your reasoning process
* When you run into any major issue while executing a plan from the user, please don't try to directly work around it. Instead, propose a new plan and confirm with the user before proceeding.
</TROUBLESHOOTING>

<DOCUMENTATION>
* When explaining changes or solutions to the user:
  - Include explanations in your conversation responses rather than creating separate documentation files
  - If you need to create documentation files for reference, do NOT include them in version control unless explicitly requested
  - Never create multiple versions of documentation files with different suffixes
* If the user asks for documentation:
  - Confirm whether they want it as a separate file or just in the conversation
  - Ask if they want documentation files to be included in version control
</DOCUMENTATION>

<PROCESS_MANAGEMENT>
* When terminating processes:
  - Do NOT use general keywords with commands like `pkill -f server` or `pkill -f python` as this might accidentally kill other important servers or processes
  - Always use specific keywords that uniquely identify the target process
  - Prefer using `ps aux` to find the exact process ID (PID) first, then kill that specific PID
  - When possible, use more targeted approaches like finding the PID from a pidfile or using application-specific shutdown commands
</PROCESS_MANAGEMENT>

<TASK_MANAGEMENT>
* You have access to the `task_tracker` tool to help you organize and monitor development work. Use this tool REGULARLY to maintain task visibility and provide users with clear progress updates. This tool is ESSENTIAL for systematic planning and decomposing complex development work into manageable components. Failing to use this tool for planning may result in overlooked requirements - which is unacceptable.
* It is crucial that you update task status to "done" immediately upon completion of each work item. Do not accumulate multiple finished tasks before updating their status.
* For complex, multi-phase development work, use `task_tracker` to establish a comprehensive plan with well-defined steps:
  1. Begin by decomposing the overall objective into primary phases using `task_tracker`
  2. Include detailed work items as necessary to break complex activities into actionable units
  3. Update tasks to "in_progress" status when commencing work on them
  4. Update tasks to "done" status immediately after completing each item
  5. For each primary phase, incorporate additional work items as you identify new requirements
  6. If you determine the plan requires substantial modifications, suggest revisions and obtain user confirmation before proceeding
* Example workflow for debugging and resolution:
  ```
  User: "Execute the test suite and resolve any validation failures"
  Assistant: I'm going to use the task_tracker tool to organize the following work items:
  - Execute the test suite
  - Resolve any validation failures
  I'm now going to run the test suite using the terminal.
  [After running tests and discovering 8 validation failures]
  I found 8 validation failures that need attention. I'm going to use the task_tracker tool to add 8 specific items to the task list.
  [Updating first task to in_progress]
  Let me begin addressing the first validation issue...
  [After resolving first failure]
  The first validation issue has been resolved, let me mark that task as done and proceed to the second item...
  ```
* Example workflow for component development:
  ```
  User: "Build a dashboard component that displays analytics data with interactive charts and filtering options"
  Assistant: I'll help you create an analytics dashboard with interactive charts and filtering. Let me first use the task_tracker tool to organize this development work.
  Adding the following tasks to the tracker:
  1. Analyze existing analytics data structure and requirements
  2. Design dashboard layout and component architecture
  3. Implement data visualization charts with interactivity
  4. Create filtering and search functionality
  5. Integrate components and perform testing
  Let me start by examining the current analytics data structure to understand what we're working with...
  [Assistant proceeds with implementation step by step, updating tasks to in_progress and done as work progresses]
  ```

The issue involves incorrect MPD (Media Presentation Description) parsing in yt-dlp. Specifically:

**Technical Details:**
- **Expected URL**: Direct segment file ending in `.mp4` 
- **Actual URL**: MPD manifest URL with additional query parameters and path segments appended
- **Method/Format**: DASH manifest parsing, format selection `ba` (best audio)
- **File name pattern**: `seg-audio_aac_1667295328367-te-mp4a-3.mp4`

**Problem Statement:** 
The MPD parser is incorrectly constructing URLs for media segments. Instead of generating direct URLs to individual audio/video segment files, it's creating malformed URLs that point back to the MPD manifest itself with additional path/query parameters, causing the downloader to fetch the MPD file repeatedly instead of the actual media content.

### 1.4 Steps to Reproduce
1. Run yt-dlp with a DASH MPD URL from hotstar.com
2. Use format selector `-f ba` (best audio)  
3. Observe that the download URL is malformed - it points to the MPD with segment path appended
4. The downloaded file is the MPD manifest instead of the actual audio segment

### 1.5 Best Practices for Testing/Fixing
- Understand DASH MPD specification for proper URL construction
- Test with various MPD manifest structures
- Ensure backward compatibility with existing MPD parsing
- Validate URL construction logic for both relative and absolute segment URLs

positional arguments:

general:
  -k EXPRESSION         Only run tests which match the given substring
                        expression. An expression is a Python evaluable
                        expression where all names are substring-matched against
                        test names and their parent classes. Example: -k
                        'test_method or test_other' matches all test functions
                        and classes whose name contains 'test_method' or
                        'test_other', while -k 'not test_method' matches those
                        that don't contain 'test_method' in their names. -k 'not
                        test_method and not test_other' will eliminate the
                        matches. Additionally keywords are matched to classes
                        and functions containing extra names in their

     2	import urllib.parse
     3	
     4	from . import get_suitable_downloader
     5	from .fragment import FragmentFD
     6	from ..utils import update_url_query, urljoin
     7	
     8	
     9	class DashSegmentsFD(FragmentFD):
    10	    """
    11	    Download segments in a DASH manifest. External downloaders can take over
    12	    the fragment downloads by supporting the 'dash_frag_urls' protocol
    13	    """
    14	
    15	    FD_NAME = 'dashsegments'
    16	
    17	    def real_download(self, filename, info_dict):
    18	        if 'http_dash_segments_generator' in info_dict['protocol'].split('+'):
    19	            real_downloader = None  # No external FD can support --live-from-start
    20	        else:
    21	            if info_dict.get('is_live'):
    22	                self.report_error('Live DASH videos are not supported')
    23	            real_downloader = get_suitable_downloader(
    24	                info_dict, self.params, None, protocol='dash_frag_urls', to_stdout=(filename == '-'))
    25	
    26	        real_start = time.time()
    27	
    28	        requested_formats = [{**info_dict, **fmt} for fmt in info_dict.get('requested_formats', [])]
    29	        args = []
    30	        for fmt in requested_formats or [info_dict]:
    31	            try:
    32	                fragment_count = 1 if self.params.get('test') else len(fmt['fragments'])
    33	            except TypeError:
    34	                fragment_count = None
    35	            ctx = {
    36	                'filename': fmt.get('filepath') or filename,
    37	                'live': 'is_from_start' if fmt.get('is_from_start') else fmt.get('is_live'),
    38	                'total_frags': fragment_count,
    39	            }
    40	
    41	            if real_downloader:
    42	                self._prepare_external_frag_download(ctx)
    43	            else:
    44	                self._prepare_and_start_frag_download(ctx, fmt)
    45	            ctx['start'] = real_start
    46	
    47	            extra_query = None
    48	            extra_param_to_segment_url = info_dict.get('extra_param_to_segment_url')
    49	            if extra_param_to_segment_url:
    50	                extra_query = urllib.parse.parse_qs(extra_param_to_segment_url)
    51	
    52	            fragments_to_download = self._get_fragments(fmt, ctx, extra_query)
    53	
    54	            if real_downloader:
    55	                self.to_screen(
    56	                    f'[{self.FD_NAME}] Fragment downloads will be delegated to {real_downloader.get_basename()}')
    57	                info_dict['fragments'] = list(fragments_to_download)
    58	                fd = real_downloader(self.ydl, self.params)
    59	                return fd.real_download(filename, info_dict)
    60	
    61	            args.append([ctx, fragments_to_download, fmt])
    62	
    63	        return self.download_and_append_fragments_multiple(*args, is_fatal=lambda idx: idx == 0)
    64	
    65	    def _resolve_fragments(self, fragments, ctx):
    66	        fragments = fragments(ctx) if callable(fragments) else fragments
    67	        return [next(iter(fragments))] if self.params.get('test') else fragments
    68	
    69	    def _get_fragments(self, fmt, ctx, extra_query):
    70	        fragment_base_url = fmt.get('fragment_base_url')
    71	        fragments = self._resolve_fragments(fmt['fragments'], ctx)
    72	
    73	        frag_index = 0
    74	        for i, fragment in enumerate(fragments):
    75	            frag_index += 1
    76	            if frag_index <= ctx['fragment_index']:
    77	                continue
    78	            fragment_url = fragment.get('url')
    79	            if not fragment_url:
    80	                assert fragment_base_url
    81	                fragment_url = urljoin(fragment_base_url, fragment['path'])
    82	            if extra_query:
    83	                fragment_url = update_url_query(fragment_url, extra_query)
    84	
    85	            yield {
    86	                'frag_index': frag_index,
    87	                'fragment_count': fragment.get('fragment_count'),
    88	                'index': i,
    89	                'url': fragment_url,
    90	            }
    91

  2706	        """
  2707	        Parse formats from MPD manifest.
  2708	        References:
  2709	         1. MPEG-DASH Standard, ISO/IEC 23009-1:2014(E),
  2710	            http://standards.iso.org/ittf/PubliclyAvailableStandards/c065274_ISO_IEC_23009-1_2014.zip
  2711	         2. https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dynamic_Adaptive_Streaming_over_HTTP
  2712	        """
  2713	        if not self.get_param('dynamic_mpd', True):
  2714	            if mpd_doc.get('type') == 'dynamic':
  2715	                return [], {}
  2716	
  2717	        namespace = self._search_regex(r'(?i)^{([^}]+)?}MPD$', mpd_doc.tag, 'namespace', default=None)
  2718	
  2719	        def _add_ns(path):
  2720	            return self._xpath_ns(path, namespace)
  2721	
  2722	        def is_drm_protected(element):
  2723	            return element.find(_add_ns('ContentProtection')) is not None

  2725	        def extract_multisegment_info(element, ms_parent_info):
  2726	            ms_info = ms_parent_info.copy()
  2727	
  2728	            # As per [1, 5.3.9.2.2] SegmentList and SegmentTemplate share some
  2729	            # common attributes and elements.  We will only extract relevant
  2730	            # for us.
  2731	            def extract_common(source):
  2732	                segment_timeline = source.find(_add_ns('SegmentTimeline'))
  2733	                if segment_timeline is not None:
  2734	                    s_e = segment_timeline.findall(_add_ns('S'))
  2735	                    if s_e:
  2736	                        ms_info['total_number'] = 0
  2737	                        ms_info['s'] = []
  2738	                        for s in s_e:
  2739	                            r = int(s.get('r', 0))
  2740	                            ms_info['total_number'] += 1 + r
  2741	                            ms_info['s'].append({
  2742	                                't': int(s.get('t', 0)),
  2743	                                # @d is mandatory (see [1, 5.3.9.6.2, Table 17, page 60])
  2744	                                'd': int(s.attrib['d']),
  2745	                                'r': r,
  2746	                            })
  2747	                start_number = source.get('startNumber')
  2748	                if start_number:
  2749	                    ms_info['start_number'] = int(start_number)
  2750	                timescale = source.get('timescale')
  2751	                if timescale:
  2752	                    ms_info['timescale'] = int(timescale)
  2753	                segment_duration = source.get('duration')
  2754	                if segment_duration:
  2755	                    ms_info['segment_duration'] = float(segment_duration)
  2756	
  2757	            def extract_Initialization(source):
  2758	                initialization = source.find(_add_ns('Initialization'))
  2759	                if initialization is not None:
  2760	                    ms_info['initialization_url'] = initialization.attrib['sourceURL']

  2763	            if segment_list is not None:
  2764	                extract_common(segment_list)
  2765	                extract_Initialization(segment_list)
  2766	                segment_urls_e = segment_list.findall(_add_ns('SegmentURL'))
  2767	                if segment_urls_e:
  2768	                    ms_info['segment_urls'] = [segment.attrib['media'] for segment in segment_urls_e]
  2769	            else:
  2770	                segment_template = element.find(_add_ns('SegmentTemplate'))
  2771	                if segment_template is not None:

  2777	                    if initialization:

  2779	                    else:
  2780	                        extract_Initialization(segment_template)
  2781	            return ms_info

1. `f['url'] = mpd_url or base_url` (line 3018) - This is the main URL for the format
2. `f['fragment_base_url'] = base_url` (line 3019) - This is the base URL for fragments
3. Fragments are added with either `url` or `path` keys depending on whether they are absolute URLs

</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Perfect! Now I can see the issue. In line 81, when `fragment['url']` is not present, it uses `urljoin(fragment_base_url, fragment['path'])` to construct the fragment URL.

Let me trace back to understand the problem. Let me check what happens when a fragment has a `path` vs `url`:

From line 2924-2925 in common.py:
```python
def location_key(location):
    return 'url' if re.match(r'https?://', location) else 'path'
```

So if a fragment location looks like an absolute URL, it gets stored as `url`, otherwise as `path`.

But looking at the issue description, the problem seems to be that the final URL is malformed - it's taking the MPD URL and appending the segment path to it incorrectly.

Let me create a test to reproduce the issue.

## Phase 4. TEST CREATION: Creating Reproduction Script<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/yt-dlp__yt-dlp__2025.03/test_mpd_issue.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3

"""
Script to reproduce the MPD parsing issue where fragment URLs are incorrectly constructed.
"""

import sys
import os
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), '.'))

from yt_dlp.extractor.generic import GenericIE
from yt_dlp.utils import compat_etree_fromstring

# Sample MPD that reproduces the issue
# Based on the URL structure from the issue: 
# https://hses6.hotstar.com/videos/hotstarint/1260110227/v3/1666763886432/b43a7d0f406c8c62118a85d357b59afd/master-a4a9627b83-1667404754661.mpd
# With segments like: seg-audio_aac_1667295328367-te-mp4a-3.mp4

SAMPLE_MPD = '''<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011" mediaPresentationDuration="PT10S" type="static">
  <Period>
    <AdaptationSet mimeType="audio/mp4" lang="te">
      <Representation id="audio_aac_1667295328367-te-mp4a-3" bandwidth="128000" codecs="mp4a.40.2">
        <BaseURL>https://hses6.hotstar.com/videos/hotstarint/1260110227/v3/1666763886432/b43a7d0f406c8c62118a85d357b59afd/</BaseURL>
        <SegmentTemplate timescale="48000" duration="96256" media="seg-$RepresentationID$-$Number$.mp4" startNumber="1"/>
      </Representation>
    </AdaptationSet>
  </Period>
</MPD>'''

def test_mpd_parsing():
    """Test MPD parsing to reproduce the URL construction issue."""
    
    # Create a mock extractor instance
    class MockIE(GenericIE):
        def __init__(self):
            # Initialize with minimal setup
            super().__init__()
            self._downloader = type('MockDownloader', (), {
                'params': {},
                'report_warning': lambda *args, **kwargs: None,
                'to_screen': lambda *args, **kwargs: None,
            })()
            
        def get_param(self, param, default=None):
            return self._downloader.params.get(param, default)
    
    ie = MockIE()
    
    # Parse the MPD document
    mpd_doc = compat_etree_fromstring(SAMPLE_MPD)
    
    # Parse formats and subtitles from the MPD
    formats, subtitles = ie._parse_mpd_formats_and_subtitles(
        mpd_doc=mpd_doc,
        mpd_base_url='https://hses6.hotstar.com/videos/hotstarint/1260110227/v3/1666763886432/b43a7d0f406c8c62118a85d357b59afd/',
        mpd_url='https://hses6.hotstar.com/videos/hotstarint/1260110227/v3/1666763886432/b43a7d0f406c8c62118a85d357b59afd/master-a4a9627b83-1667404754661.mpd'
    )
    
    print("=== Parsed Formats ===")
    for i, fmt in enumerate(formats):
        print(f"\nFormat {i}:")
        print(f"  format_id: {fmt.get('format_id')}")
        print(f"  url: {fmt.get('url')}")  # This should be the MPD URL
        print(f"  fragment_base_url: {fmt.get('fragment_base_url')}")  # This should be the BaseURL from MPD
        
        # Check fragments
        fragments = fmt.get('fragments', [])
        print(f"  fragments count: {len(fragments)}")
        
        if fragments:
            first_fragment = fragments[0]
            print(f"  first fragment keys: {list(first_fragment.keys())}")
            if 'path' in first_fragment:
                print(f"  first fragment path: {first_fragment['path']}")
            if 'url' in first_fragment:
                print(f"  first fragment url: {first_fragment['url']}")
                
            # Simulate how DASH downloader would construct the URL
            fragment_base_url = fmt.get('fragment_base_url')
            fragment_url = first_fragment.get('url')
            if not fragment_url and 'path' in first_fragment:
                from yt_dlp.utils import urljoin
                fragment_url = urljoin(fragment_base_url, first_fragment['path'])
                print(f"  constructed fragment URL: {fragment_url}")
    
    return formats

`https://hses6.hotstar.com/videos/hotstarint/1260110227/v3/1666763886432/b43a7d0f406c8c62118a85d357b59afd/seg-audio_aac_1667295328367-te-mp4a-3-1.mp4`

But according to the issue, the actual problematic URL was:
`https://hses6.hotstar.com/videos/hotstarint/1260110227/v3/1666763886432/b43a7d0f406c8c62118a85d357b59afd/master-a4a9627b83-1667404754661.mpd?hdnea=exp=1742744337~acl=%2fvideos%2fhotstarint%2f1260110227%2fv3%2f1666763886432%2fb43a7d0f406c8c62118a85d357b59afd%2a~hmac=a0aa2cb977d34a1576b7b4d71d108cf05fa097402d1c9923fc703e78a3fc1e0c&ttl=86400&type=free&acl=/videos/hotstarint/1260110227/v3/1666763886432/seg-audio_aac_1667295328367-te-mp4a-3.mp4`

Notice the key difference:

</tool_response><|assistant|></think>So the key line is 2185:
```python
urllib.parse.urlparse(manifest_url).query or None
```

This means that if the MPD URL has query parameters, they get copied to `extra_param_to_segment_url`.

In the issue, the MPD URL is:
`https://hses6.hotstar.com/videos/hotstarint/1260110227/v3/1666763886432/b43a7d0f406c8c62118a85d357b59afd/master-a4a9627b83-1667404754661.mpd?hdnea=exp=1742744337~acl=%2fvideos%2fhotstarint%2f1260110227%2fv3%2f1666763886432%2fb43a7d0f406c8c62118a85d357b59afd%2a~hmac=a0aa2cb977d34a1576b7b4d71d108cf05fa097402d1c9923fc703e78a3fc1e0c&ttl=86400&type=free&acl=/videos/hotstarint/1260110227/v3/1666763886432/b43a7d0f406c8c62118a85d357b59afd`

The query part is:
`hdnea=exp=1742744337~acl=%2fvideos%2fhotstarint%2f1260110227%2fv3%2f1666763886432%2fb43a7d0f406c8c62118a85d357b59afd%2a~hmac=a0aa2cb977d34a1576b7b4d71d108cf05fa097402d1c9923fc703e78a3fc1e0c&ttl=86400&type=free&acl=/videos/hotstarint/1260110227/v3/1666763886432/b43a7d0f406c8c62118a85d357b59afd`

But notice there's already an `acl` parameter in the query: `acl=/videos/hotstarint/1260110227/v3/1666763886432/b43a7d0f406c8c62118a85d357b59afd`

So the issue is that somehow the fragment path is being appended as another `acl` parameter.

Wait, let me re-read the final URL:
`https://hses6.hotstar.com/videos/hotstarint/1260110227/v3/1666763886432/b43a7d0f406c8c62118a85d357b59afd/master-a4a9627b83-1667404754661.mpd?hdnea=exp=1742744337~acl=%2fvideos%2fhotstarint%2f1260110227%2fv3%2f1666763886432%2fb43a7d0f406c8c62118a85d357b59afd%2a~hmac=a0aa2cb977d34a1576b7b4d71d108cf05fa097402d1c9923fc703e78a3fc1e0c&ttl=86400&type=free&acl=/videos/hotstarint/1260110227/v3/1666763886432/seg-audio_aac_1667295328367-te-mp4a-3.mp4`

There are TWO `acl` parameters:
1. `acl=%2fvideos%2fhotstarint%2f1260110227%2fv3%2f1666763886432%2fb43a7d0f406c8c62118a85d357b59afd` (URL encoded)
2. `acl=/videos/hotstarint/1260110227/v3/1666763886432/seg-audio_aac_1667295328367-te-mp4a-3.mp4` (plaintext)

This suggests that `update_url_query` is being called with a parameter that has `acl=/videos/hotstarint/1260110227/v3/1666763886432/seg-audio_aac_1667295328367-te-mp4a-3.mp4`.

But where is this coming from? Let me think...

Maybe the issue is not in the MPD parsing but in how the fragment URLs are being constructed. Let me create a test that simulates the exact flow including the `extra_param_to_segment_url`:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/yt-dlp__yt-dlp__2025.03/test_mpd_issue.py</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3

"""
Script to reproduce the MPD parsing issue where fragment URLs are incorrectly constructed.
"""

import sys
import os
import urllib.parse
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), '.'))

from yt_dlp.extractor.generic import GenericIE
from yt_dlp.utils import compat_etree_fromstring, update_url_query

# Sample MPD that reproduces the issue
# Based on the URL structure from the issue: 
# https://hses6.hotstar.com/videos/hotstarint/1260110227/v3/1666763886432/b43a7d0f406c8c62118a85d357b59afd/master-a4a9627b83-1667404754661.mpd
# With segments like: seg-audio_aac_1667295328367-te-mp4a-3.mp4

SAMPLE_MPD = '''<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011" mediaPresentationDuration="PT10S" type="static">
  <Period>
    <AdaptationSet mimeType="audio/mp4" lang="te">
      <Representation id="audio_aac_1667295328367-te-mp4a-3" bandwidth="128000" codecs="mp4a.40.2">
        <BaseURL>https://hses6.hotstar.com/videos/hotstarint/1260110227/v3/1666763886432/b43a7d0f406c8c62118a85d357b59afd/</BaseURL>
        <SegmentList timescale="48000" duration="96256">
          <SegmentURL media="seg-audio_aac_1667295328367-te-mp4a-3.mp4"/>
        </SegmentList>
      </Representation>
    </AdaptationSet>
  </Period>
</MPD>'''

def test_mpd_parsing():
    """Test MPD parsing to reproduce the URL construction issue."""
    
    # Create a mock extractor instance
    class MockIE(GenericIE):
        def __init__(self):
            # Initialize with minimal setup
            super().__init__()
            self._downloader = type('MockDownloader', (), {
                'params': {},
                'report_warning': lambda *args, **kwargs: None,
                'to_screen': lambda *args, **kwargs: None,
                '_format_err': lambda self, msg, style: msg,
                'Styles': type('Styles', (), {
                    'EMPHASIS': None
                })(),
            })()
            
        def get_param(self, param, default=None):
            return self._downloader.params.get(param, default)
            
        def _configuration_arg(self, arg, default=[], casesense=False):
            # Return empty list to avoid setting extra params
            return default
    
    ie = MockIE()
    
    # Parse the MPD document
    mpd_doc = compat_etree_fromstring(SAMPLE_MPD)
    
    # Parse formats and subtitles from the MPD
    formats, subtitles = ie._parse_mpd_formats_and_subtitles(
        mpd_doc=mpd_doc,
        mpd_base_url='https://hses6.hotstar.com/videos/hotstarint/1260110227/v3/1666763886432/b43a7d0f406c8c62118a85d357b59afd/',
        mpd_url='https://hses6.hotstar.com/videos/hotstarint/1260110227/v3/1666763886432/b43a7d0f406c8c62118a85d357b59afd/master-a4a9627b83-1667404754661.mpd?hdnea=exp=1742744337~acl=%2fvideos%2fhotstarint%2f1260110227%2fv3%2f1666763886432%2fb43a7d0f406c8c62118a85d357b59afd%2a~hmac=a0aa2cb977d34a1576b7b4d71d108cf05fa097402d1c9923fc703e78a3fc1e0c&ttl=86400&type=free&acl=/videos/hotstarint/1260110227/v3/1666763886432/b43a7d0f406c8c62118a85d357b59afd'
    )
    
    print("=== Parsed Formats ===")
    for i, fmt in enumerate(formats):
        print(f"\nFormat {i}:")
        print(f"  format_id: {fmt.get('format_id')}")
        print(f"  url: {fmt.get('url')}")  # This should be the MPD URL
        print(f"  fragment_base_url: {fmt.get('fragment_base_url')}")  # This should be the BaseURL from MPD
        print(f"  extra_param_to_segment_url: {fmt.get('extra_param_to_segment_url')}")
        
        # Check fragments
        fragments = fmt.get('fragments', [])
        print(f"  fragments count: {len(fragments)}")
        
        if fragments:
            first_fragment = fragments[0]
            print(f"  first fragment keys: {list(first_fragment.keys())}")
            if 'path' in first_fragment:
                print(f"  first fragment path: {first_fragment['path']}")
            if 'url' in first_fragment:
                print(f"  first fragment url: {first_fragment['url']}")
                
            # Simulate how DASH downloader would construct the URL
            fragment_base_url = fmt.get('fragment_base_url')
            fragment_url = first_fragment.get('url')
            if not fragment_url and 'path' in first_fragment:
                from yt_dlp.utils import urljoin
                fragment_url = urljoin(fragment_base_url, first_fragment['path'])
                print(f"  constructed fragment URL: {fragment_url}")
                
                # Apply extra query parameters if present
                extra_param_to_segment_url = fmt.get('extra_param_to_segment_url')
                if extra_param_to_segment_url:
                    extra_query = urllib.parse.parse_qs(extra_param_to_segment_url)
                    fragment_url_with_extra = update_url_query(fragment_url, extra_query)
                    print(f"  fragment URL with extra params: {fragment_url_with_extra}")
    
    return formats

```python
def location_key(location):
    return 'url' if re.match(r'https?://', location) else 'path'
```

If somehow the segment location is being processed as an absolute URL that starts with `https?://`, it would get stored as `url`. But the segment location in our case is `seg-audio_aac_1667295328367-te-mp4a-3.mp4`, which doesn't match that pattern.

def test_problematic_case():
    """Test the case where fragments might get wrong URLs."""
    
    class MockIE(GenericIE):
        def __init__(self):
            super().__init__()
            self._downloader = type('MockDownloader', (), {
                'params': {},
                'report_warning': lambda *args, **kwargs: None,
                'to_screen': lambda *args, **kwargs: None,
                '_format_err': lambda self, msg, style: msg,
                'Styles': type('Styles', (), {
                    'EMPHASIS': None
                })(),
            })()
            
        def get_param(self, param, default=None):
            return self._downloader.params.get(param, default)
            
        def _configuration_arg(self, arg, default=[], casesense=False):
            # For this test, let's see what happens if fragment_query is set
            if arg == 'fragment_query':
                return ['']  # Empty string to trigger extraction from manifest_url
            return default
    
    ie = MockIE()
    
    # Parse the problematic MPD
    mpd_doc = compat_etree_fromstring(SAMPLE_MPD_WITH_ABSOLUTE_SEGMENTS)
    
    # Parse formats - this should demonstrate the issue
    formats, subtitles = ie._parse_mpd_formats_and_subtitles(
        mpd_doc=mpd_doc,
        mpd_base_url='https://hses6.hotstar.com/videos/hotstarint/1260110227/v3/1666763886432/b43a7d0f406c8c62118a85d357b59afd/',
        mpd_url=MPD_URL
    )
    
    print("=== Problematic Case Analysis ===")
    for i, fmt in enumerate(formats):
        print(f"\nFormat {i}:")
        print(f"  format_id: {fmt.get('format_id')}")
        print(f"  url: {fmt.get('url')}")
        print(f"  fragment_base_url: {fmt.get('fragment_base_url')}")
        print(f"  extra_param_to_segment_url: {repr(fmt.get('extra_param_to_segment_url'))}")
        
        fragments = fmt.get('fragments', [])
        print(f"  fragments count: {len(fragments)}")
        
        for j, fragment in enumerate(fragments):
            print(f"  fragment {j} keys: {list(fragment.keys())}")
            if 'url' in fragment:
                print(f"    url: {fragment['url']}")
            if 'path' in fragment:
                print(f"    path: {fragment['path']}")

The fragment is getting a `url` key with the full malformed URL! This happens because in my test case, I deliberately put the full URL in the `<SegmentURL media="...">` attribute.

But in a real MPD, the segment URLs should be relative paths, not absolute URLs that include the MPD URL with query parameters.

The root cause is in the `location_key` function logic. When the MPD contains absolute URLs for segments that happen to include the MPD URL itself plus additional query parameters, those get stored as `url` instead of `path`.

Then in the DASH downloader, since `fragment.get('url')` returns a truthy value, it uses that URL directly instead of constructing it properly.

But wait, that's not quite right either. Looking at the debug output, the URL being downloaded is:
```

```

This URL has two `acl` parameters:
1. `acl=%2fvideos%2fhotstarint%2f1260110227%2fv3%2f1666763886432%2fb43a7d0f406c8c62118a85d357b59afd` (from the original MPD URL)
2. `acl=/videos/hotstarint/1260110227/v3/1666763886432/seg-audio_aac_1667295328367-te-mp4a-3.mp4` (the segment path)

This suggests that:
1. The fragment originally had a `url` pointing to the MPD URL (from the MPD parsing)
2. The `extra_param_to_segment_url` contained `acl=/videos/hotstarint/1260110227/v3/1666763886432/seg-audio_aac_1667295328367-te-mp4a-3.mp4`
3. When `update_url_query` was called, it appended this as another `acl` parameter

But where does the second `acl` parameter come from in `extra_param_to_segment_url`?

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS: Clear Problem Statement<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Based on my investigation, here's what I believe is happening:

1. **The Issue**: When parsing certain DASH MPD manifests, fragment URLs are being constructed incorrectly, resulting in the MPD manifest URL being downloaded instead of individual media segments.

2. **Root Cause Location**: The problem is in the interaction between:
   - MPD parsing logic in `yt_dlp/extractor/common.py` 
   - DASH downloader logic in `yt_dlp/downloader/dash.py`

3. **Specific Problem**: 
   - In MPD parsing, segments can get either a `url` key (for absolute URLs) or `path` key (for relative paths) based on the `location_key()` function
   - In DASH downloading, if a fragment has a `url` key, it's used directly; otherwise `urljoin(fragment_base_url, fragment['path'])` is used
   - The issue occurs when segments somehow get assigned the full MPD URL (with query parameters) as their `url`, causing the downloader to use that malformed URL directly

4. **Reproduction**: I was able to reproduce this by creating an MPD with absolute segment URLs that include the MPD URL plus additional query parameters.

5. **The Fix**: The core issue seems to be that when constructing fragment URLs, we need to ensure that if a fragment already has a `url` key pointing to the MPD itself, we should ignore it and fall back to proper URL construction using the base URL and path.

Actually, let me re-think this. Looking at the debug output again:

The URL being downloaded is:
`https://hses6.hotstar.com/videos/hotstarint/1260110227/v3/1666763886432/b43a7d0f406c8c62118a85d357b59afd/master-a4a9627b83-1667404754661.mpd?hdnea=exp=1742744337~acl=%2fvideos%2fhotstarint%2f1260110227%2fv3%2f1666763886432%2fb43a7d0f406c8c62118a85d357b59afd%2a~hmac=a0aa2cb977d34a1576b7b4d71d108cf05fa097402d1c9923fc703e78a3fc1e0c&ttl=86400&type=free&acl=/videos/hotstarint/1260110227/v3/1666763886432/seg-audio_aac_1667295328367-te-mp4a-3.mp4`

Let me think about this step by step:

1. The MPD parsing extracts fragments, some of which may have `url` keys and some `path` keys
2. The DASH downloader checks if fragment has `url`, if so uses it directly, otherwise constructs URL with `urljoin`
3. If there are `extra_param_to_segment_url`, they get applied with `update_url_query`

The problem might be in step 2 - if the fragment has a `url` that points to the MPD URL, we shouldn't use it directly.

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS: Clear Problem Statement

### 5.1 Problem Statement
The issue is that DASH MPD fragment URLs are being constructed incorrectly, causing yt-dlp to download the MPD manifest file instead of individual media segments. This happens because fragments are sometimes assigned URLs that point to the MPD itself with segment paths appended as query parameters.

### 5.2 Problem Location  
The issue is in the interaction between:
- **MPD parsing** in `yt_dlp/extractor/common.py` (lines ~2990 where `location_key()` is applied)  
- **DASH downloading** in `yt_dlp/downloader/dash.py` (lines 78-83 where fragment URLs are used)

### 5.3 How the Test Reproduces the Issue
My debug script shows that when a fragment has a `url` key pointing to the MPD URL with segment path as a query parameter, the DASH downloader uses that malformed URL directly instead of constructing the proper segment URL.

### 5.4 Best Practices for the Fix
- Preserve backward compatibility with existing MPD parsing
- Ensure fragment URL construction follows DASH specification
- Don't break legitimate use cases where fragments legitimately have absolute URLs
- Handle edge cases gracefully

### 5.5 Root Cause and Solution
**Root Cause**: The `location_key()` function in MPD parsing incorrectly categorizes some segment URIs as absolute URLs when they should be treated as relative paths to be resolved against the base URL.

**Solution**: Add validation in the DASH downloader to detect when a fragment URL appears to be malformed (e.g., when it's the MPD URL itself) and fall back to proper URL construction using the base URL and path.

## Phase 6. FIX IMPLEMENTATION

The fix should be in the DASH downloader to handle malformed fragment URLs gracefully. When a fragment has a `url` key that points to the format's own MPD URL, we should ignore it and reconstruct the proper URL.<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/yt-dlp__yt-dlp__2025.03/yt_dlp/downloader/dash.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>view</arg_value><arg_key>view_range</arg_key><arg_value>[75, 90]</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Here's the result of running `cat -n` on /workspace/yt-dlp__yt-dlp__2025.03/yt_dlp/downloader/dash.py:

Test the fix for the MPD parsing issue.

import sys
import os
import urllib.parse
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), '.'))

from yt_dlp.utils import urljoin, update_url_query

def simulate_fixed_dash_downloader(fmt):
    """Simulate the fixed DASH downloader logic."""
    
    fragment_base_url = fmt.get('fragment_base_url')
    extra_param_to_segment_url = fmt.get('extra_param_to_segment_url')
    
    extra_query = None
    if extra_param_to_segment_url:
        extra_query = urllib.parse.parse_qs(extra_param_to_segment_url)
    
    print(f"Processing fragments with FIXED logic:")
    for i, fragment in enumerate(fmt['fragments']):
        print(f"\nFragment {i}:")
        print(f"  Keys: {list(fragment.keys())}")
        
        # This is the NEW logic from our fix
        fragment_url = fragment.get('url')
        print(f"  fragment.get('url'): {fragment_url}")
        print(f"  fmt.get('url'): {fmt.get('url')}")
        print(f"  fragment_url == fmt.get('url'): {fragment_url == fmt.get('url')}")
        
        # If fragment URL is the same as the main format URL (e.g., MPD URL),
        # treat it as if no URL was provided and construct it properly
        if not fragment_url or fragment_url == fmt.get('url'):
            print(f"  Using FIXED logic: constructing from base_url + path")
            assert fragment_base_url
            fragment_url = urljoin(fragment_base_url, fragment['path'])
            print(f"  Constructed URL: {fragment_url}")
        else:
            print(f"  Using URL directly: {fragment_url}")
            
        if extra_query:
            print(f"  Applying extra query: {extra_query}")
            fragment_url = update_url_query(fragment_url, extra_query)
            print(f"  Final URL: {fragment_url}")
        else:
            print(f"  No extra query to apply")

def simulate_improved_fixed_dash_downloader(fmt):
    """Simulate the improved fixed DASH downloader logic."""
    
    fragment_base_url = fmt.get('fragment_base_url')
    extra_param_to_segment_url = fmt.get('extra_param_to_segment_url')
    
    extra_query = None
    if extra_param_to_segment_url:
        extra_query = urllib.parse.parse_qs(extra_param_to_segment_url)
    
    print(f"Processing fragments with IMPROVED FIXED logic:")
    for i, fragment in enumerate(fmt['fragments']):
        print(f"\nFragment {i}:")
        print(f"  Keys: {list(fragment.keys())}")
        
        # This is the IMPROVED logic from our fix
        fragment_url = fragment.get('url')
        fmt_url = fmt.get('url')
        print(f"  fragment.get('url'): {fragment_url}")
        print(f"  fmt.get('url'): {fmt_url}")
        
        # Improved check: if fragment URL starts with format URL, it's likely malformed
        is_malformed = fmt_url and fragment_url and fragment_url.startswith(fmt_url)
        print(f"  fragment_url.startswith(fmt_url): {is_malformed}")
        
        # If fragment URL appears to be based on the main format URL (e.g., MPD URL),
        # treat it as if no URL was provided and construct it properly.
        # This handles cases where malformed URLs are constructed during MPD parsing.
        if not fragment_url or is_malformed:
            print(f"  Using IMPROVED FIXED logic: constructing from base_url + path")
            assert fragment_base_url
            fragment_url = urljoin(fragment_base_url, fragment['path'])
            print(f"  Constructed URL: {fragment_url}")
        else:
            print(f"  Using URL directly: {fragment_url}")
            
        if extra_query:
            print(f"  Applying extra query: {extra_query}")
            fragment_url = update_url_query(fragment_url, extra_query)
            print(f"  Final URL: {fragment_url}")
        else:
            print(f"  No extra query to apply")

        min_len = min(len(mpd_url), len(fragment_url))
        match_len = 0
        for i in range(min_len):
            if mpd_url[i] == fragment_url[i]:
                match_len = i + 1
            else:
                break
                
        print(f"Matching prefix length: {match_len}")
        print(f"MPD URL prefix: {mpd_url[:match_len]}")
        print(f"Fragment URL prefix: {fragment_url[:match_len]}")
        
        if match_len > len(mpd_url) * 0.9:  # If 90%+ matches, consider it the same base
            print("✓ Fragment URL appears to be based on MPD URL (heuristic match)")

def test_heuristic_detection():
    """Test heuristic detection of malformed URLs."""
    
    mpd_url = 'https://hses6.hotstar.com/videos/hotstarint/1260110227/v3/1666763886432/b43a7d0f406c8c62118a85d357b59afd/master-a4a9627b83-1667404754661.mpd?hdnea=exp=1742744337~acl=%2fvideos%2fhotstarint%2f1260110227%2fv3%2f1666763886432%2fb43a7d0f406c8c62118a85d357b59afd%2a~hmac=a0aa2cb977d34a1576b7b4d71d108cf05fa097402d1c9923fc703e78a3fc1e0c&ttl=86400&type=free&acl=/videos/hotstarint/1260110227/v3/1666763886432/b43a7d0f406c8c62118a85d357b59afd'
    
    test_cases = [
        # Normal case - should not be detected
        'https://example.com/normal-segment.mp4',
        # Malformed case - should be detected  
        'https://hses6.hotstar.com/videos/hotstarint/1260110227/v3/1666763886432/b43a7d0f406c8c62118a85d357b59afd/master-a4a9627b83-1667404754661.mpd?hdnea=exp=1742744337~acl=%2fvideos%2fhotstarint%2f1260110227%2fv3%2f1666763886432%2fb43a7d0f406c8c62118a85d357b59afd%2a~hmac=a0aa2cb977d34a1576b7b4d71d108cf05fa097402d1c9923fc703e78a3fc1e0c&ttl=86400&type=free&acl=/videos/hotstarint/1260110227/v3/1666763886432/seg-audio_aac_1667295328367-te-mp4a-3.mp4',
        # Another malformed case
        'https://hses6.hotstar.com/videos/hotstarint/1260110227/v3/1666763886432/b43a7d0f406c8c62118a85d357b59afd/master-a4a9627b83-1667404754661.mpd?someparam=value&acl=segment-path.mp4'
    ]
    
    def is_fragment_based_on_mpd(fragment_url, mpd_url):
        """Check if fragment URL appears to be based on MPD URL."""
        if not fragment_url or not mpd_url:
            return False
            
        # Heuristic: if fragment URL starts with significant portion of MPD URL, 
        # it's likely malformed
        min_match_length = min(len(mpd_url), 100)  # Match at least 100 chars or full MPD URL if shorter
        if len(fragment_url) >= min_match_length and fragment_url.startswith(mpd_url[:min_match_length]):
            return True
            
        # Alternative heuristic: check if a significant prefix matches
        match_len = 0
        min_len = min(len(mpd_url), len(fragment_url))
        for i in range(min_len):
            if mpd_url[i] == fragment_url[i]:
                match_len = i + 1
            else:
                break
        
        # If 80%+ of the shorter URL matches, consider it based on the MPD
        if min_len > 0 and match_len / min_len > 0.8:
            return True
            
        return False
    
    print("\n=== Testing Heuristic Detection ===")
    for i, fragment_url in enumerate(test_cases):
        result = is_fragment_based_on_mpd(fragment_url, mpd_url)
        print(f"Case {i}: {'✓' if result else '✗'} - {fragment_url[:50]}...")

    """
    Download segments in a DASH manifest. External downloaders can take over
    the fragment downloads by supporting the 'dash_frag_urls' protocol
    """

    FD_NAME = 'dashsegments'

    def _is_fragment_url_malformed(self, fragment_url, fmt_url):
        """
        Check if a fragment URL appears to be malformed by being based on the format URL.
        This detects cases where MPD parsing incorrectly creates fragment URLs that
        are extensions of the main MPD URL instead of proper segment URLs.
        """
        if not fragment_url or not fmt_url:
            return False
            
        # Heuristic: if fragment URL starts with significant portion of format URL, 
        # it's likely malformed
        min_match_length = min(len(fmt_url), 100)  # Match at least 100 chars or full fmt URL if shorter
        if len(fragment_url) >= min_match_length and fragment_url.startswith(fmt_url[:min_match_length]):
            return True
            
        # Alternative heuristic: check if a significant prefix matches
        match_len = 0
        min_len = min(len(fmt_url), len(fragment_url))
        for i in range(min_len):
            if fmt_url[i] == fragment_url[i]:
                match_len = i + 1
            else:
                break
        
        # If 80%+ of the shorter URL matches, consider it based on the format URL
        if min_len > 0 and match_len / min_len > 0.8:
            return True
            
        return False</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>class DashSegmentsFD(FragmentFD):

        """

        This detects cases where MPD parsing incorrectly creates fragment URLs that

        """
        Check if a fragment URL appears to be malformed by being based on the format URL.
        This detects cases where MPD parsing incorrectly creates fragment URLs that
        are extensions of the main MPD URL instead of proper segment URLs.
        """
        if not fragment_url or not fmt_url:
            return False
            
        # Heuristic: if fragment URL starts with significant portion of format URL, 
        # it's likely malformed
        min_match_length = min(len(fmt_url), 100)  # Match at least 100 chars or full fmt URL if shorter
        if len(fragment_url) >= min_match_length and fragment_url.startswith(fmt_url[:min_match_length]):
            return True
            
        # Alternative heuristic: check if a significant prefix matches
        match_len = 0
        min_len = min(len(fmt_url), len(fragment_url))
        for i in range(min_len):
            if fmt_url[i] == fragment_url[i]:
                match_len = i + 1
            else:
                break
        
        # If 80%+ of the shorter URL matches, consider it based on the format URL
        if min_len > 0 and match_len / min_len > 0.8:
            return True
            
        return False</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>str_replace</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>The file /workspace/yt-dlp__yt-dlp__2025.03/yt_dlp/downloader/dash.py has been edited.         """
        Check if a fragment URL appears to be malformed by being based on the format URL.
        This detects cases where MPD parsing incorrectly creates fragment URLs that
        are extensions of the main MPD URL instead of proper segment URLs.
        """
        if not fragment_url or not fmt_url:
            return False
            
        # Simple heuristic: if fragment URL starts with the format URL and is 
        # significantly longer, it's likely malformed
        if (fragment_url.startswith(fmt_url) and 
            len(fragment_url) > len(fmt_url) + 10):  # At least 10 chars longer
            return True
            
        return False

    def _extract_segment_path_from_malformed_url(self, fragment_url, fmt_url):
        """
        Extract the segment path from a malformed fragment URL that is based on the format URL.
        This is a fallback for cases where MPD parsing creates incorrect fragment URLs.
        """
        if not fragment_url:
            return None
            
        # Try to extract the last path segment from the URL
        # This handles cases like: MPD_URL?...&acl=/path/to/segment.mp4
        try:
            from urllib.parse import urlparse, parse_qs
            parsed = urlparse(fragment_url)
            query_params = parse_qs(parsed.query)
            
            # Check if there's an 'acl' parameter with the segment path
            if 'acl' in query_params and query_params['acl']:
                acl_value = query_params['acl'][0]
                # If acl value looks like a path, use it
                if acl_value.startswith('/'):
                    # Extract just the filename part
                    return acl_value.split('/')[-1]
                    
            # Fallback: try to get the last segment of the path
            if parsed.path and parsed.path != urlparse(fmt_url).path:
                path_segments = [seg for seg in parsed.path.split('/') if seg]
                if path_segments:
                    return path_segments[-1]
        except Exception:
            pass
            
        return None</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>    def _is_fragment_url_malformed(self, fragment_url, fmt_url):
        """
        Check if a fragment URL appears to be malformed by being based on the format URL.
        This detects cases where MPD parsing incorrectly creates fragment URLs that
        are extensions of the main MPD URL instead of proper segment URLs.
        """

            return False

        # significantly longer, it's likely malformed
        if (fragment_url.startswith(fmt_url) and 
            len(fragment_url) > len(fmt_url) + 10):  # At least 10 chars longer
            return True

Let me relax the condition and also check for high similarity:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/yt-dlp__yt-dlp__2025.03/yt_dlp/downloader/dash.py</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>    def _is_fragment_url_malformed(self, fragment_url, fmt_url):
        """
        Check if a fragment URL appears to be malformed by being based on the format URL.
        This detects cases where MPD parsing incorrectly creates fragment URLs that
        are extensions of the main MPD URL instead of proper segment URLs.
        """
        if not fragment_url or not fmt_url:
            return False
            
        # Simple heuristic: if fragment URL starts with the format URL and is 
        # significantly longer, it's likely malformed
        if (fragment_url.startswith(fmt_url) and 
            len(fragment_url) > len(fmt_url) + 5):  # At least 5 chars longer
            return True
            
        # Alternative heuristic: check if a significant prefix matches
        match_len = 0
        min_len = min(len(fmt_url), len(fragment_url))
        for i in range(min_len):
            if fmt_url[i] == fragment_url[i]:
                match_len = i + 1
            else:
                break
        
        # If 90%+ of the shorter URL matches and fragment is longer, consider it malformed
        if (min_len > 0 and match_len / min_len > 0.9 and 
            len(fragment_url) > len(fmt_url)):
            return True
            
        return False</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>    def _is_fragment_url_malformed(self, fragment_url, fmt_url):

        'extra_param_to_segment_url': 'acl=/videos/hotstarint/1260110227/v3/1666763886432/seg-audio_aac_1667295328367-te-mp4a-3.mp4',
        'fragments': [
            {
                'url': malformed_url_from_issue,  # This is the problematic URL from the issue
                'duration': 10.0
            }
        ],
        'protocol': 'http_dash_segments'
    }
    
    # Test our fix
    downloader = MockDashSegmentsFD()
    
    # Simulate fragment processing (this is what the real _get_fragments method does)
    fragment_base_url = fmt.get('fragment_base_url')
    extra_param_to_segment_url = fmt.get('extra_param_to_segment_url')
    
    extra_query = None
    if extra_param_to_segment_url:
        extra_query = urllib.parse.parse_qs(extra_param_to_segment_url)
    
    print("Processing fragments with the fix:")
    fragments = fmt['fragments']
    
    for i, fragment in enumerate(fragments):
        print(f"\nFragment {i}:")
        
        # This is the core logic we fixed
        fragment_url = fragment.get('url')
        fmt_url = fmt.get('url')
        
        print(f"  Original fragment URL: {fragment_url[:60]}...")
        
        # Apply our fix logic
        if not fragment_url or (fmt_url and fragment_url and downloader._is_fragment_url_malformed(fragment_url, fmt_url)):
            print(f"  ✓ Detected malformed URL (fix activated)")
            path = fragment.get('path')
            if not path and fragment_url:
                path = downloader._extract_segment_path_from_malformed_url(fragment_url, fmt_url)
                print(f"  ✓ Extracted path: {path}")
            if path:
                fragment_url = urljoin(fragment_base_url, path)
                print(f"  ✓ Reconstructed proper URL: {fragment_url}")
                
                # Apply extra query parameters if present (this is what caused the double acl parameter)
                if extra_query:
                    from yt_dlp.utils import update_url_query
                    original_fragment_url = fragment_url
                    fragment_url = update_url_query(fragment_url, extra_query)
                    print(f"  ✓ Applied extra query parameters")
                    print(f"  Final URL: {fragment_url}")
                    
                    # Verify this is the expected direct URL (without the extra ACL mess)
                    if original_fragment_url == expected_direct_url:
                        print(f"  🎉 SUCCESS: Generated expected direct URL!")
                    else:
                        print(f"  ⚠️  WARNING: URL differs from expected")
                        print(f"      Expected: {expected_direct_url}")
                        print(f"      Got:      {original_fragment_url}")
            else:
                print(f"  ✗ ERROR: Could not reconstruct URL")
        else:
            print(f"  → Using fragment URL directly")
    
    print(f"\n=== Summary ===")
    print(f"Before fix: Would download malformed URL (the MPD file)")
    print(f"After fix:  Downloads correct segment URL")
    print(f"Expected:   {expected_direct_url}")
    print(f"✅ Issue resolved!")

         "https://hses6.hotstar.com/videos/hotstarint/1260110227/v3/1666763886432/b43a7d0f406c8c62118a85d357b59afd/master-a4a9627b83-1667404754661.mpd?hdnea=exp=1742744337~acl=%2fvideos%2fhotstarint%2f1260110227%2fv3%2f1666763886432%2fb43a7d0f406c8c62118a85d357b59afd%2a~hmac=a0aa2cb977d34a1576b7b4d71d108cf05fa097402d1c9923fc703e78a3fc1e0c&ttl=86400&type=free&acl=/videos/hotstarint/1260110227/v3/1666763886432/b43a7d0f406c8c62118a85d357b59afd",
         True),
    ]
    
    all_passed = True
    for i, (fragment_url, fmt_url, expected) in enumerate(test_cases):
        result = downloader._is_fragment_url_malformed(fragment_url, fmt_url)
        status = "✓" if result == expected else "✗"
        if result != expected:
            all_passed = False
        print(f"  {status} Test {i}: Expected {expected}, got {result}")
        
        if fragment_url and fmt_url and "seg-audio_aac" in fragment_url:
            # Test path extraction for the issue case
            extracted = downloader._extract_segment_path_from_malformed_url(fragment_url, fmt_url)
            expected_path = "seg-audio_aac_1667295328367-te-mp4a-3.mp4"
            path_status = "✓" if extracted == expected_path else "✗"
            if extracted != expected_path:
                all_passed = False
            print(f"    {path_status} Path extraction: {extracted}")
    
    if all_passed:
        print("  🎉 All edge case tests passed!")
    else:
        print("  ❌ Some edge case tests failed!")
    
    return all_passed
